Services](../../../services/services-overview/)\\n* [ Contributors](../../../contributors/docs-style/)\\n\\n* [Home](../../../)\\n* [Guides](./)\\n* Getting started\\n* First steps\\n\\n# Sign up and try CircleCI\\n\\n[  2 months ago](https://github.com/circleci/circleci-docs/commit/0c04b7910fb0d21748929dedff736403016b6903) \\n\\nCloud \\n\\nMarkdown \\n* [ View markdown](index.md)\\n* Copy markdown\\n\\nTo start building on CircleCI, go to the [Sign Up](https://circleci.com/signup/) page or follow an email invite link from your team, and create an account. After signing up you can create an organization or find your teammates.\\n\\nCircleCI supports GitHub, Bitbucket, and GitLab projects.\\n\\nIf you use **GitHub**, **GitLab**, or **Bitbucket Data Center** and are creating a CircleCI organization for your team, you will set the version control system (VCS) integration type, and authenticate with your VCS provider during the signup process. Team members that you invite to your CircleCI org can join without authenticating with your VCS. However, if they are to create new projects, they will authenticate during the project creation process.\\n\\nIf you use **Bitbucket Cloud**, every team member signing up for a new account and joining your org will authenticate with Bitbucket _during_ the signup process.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up)Sign up\\n\\nFrom the [Sign Up](https://circleci.com/signup/) page, do one of the following:\\n\\n### [](#github-gitlab-bitbucket-data-center)Building GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket Data Center projects?\\n\\nIf you want to build mainly **GitHub**, **GitLab** or **Bitbucket Data Center** projects, sign up with your email address to create an account.\\n\\n| |  If you want to collaborate on an existing org you can create an account and request an invite later. |\\n| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\n\\n1. Select **Sign Up**.\\n2. Enter your email address and create a password.\\n3. Answer a few questions about how you plan to use CircleCI.\\n4. You can now choose one of the following:  \\n   * Use the [Find Your Team](../invite-your-team/) option to connect your account to a shared org  \\n   * [Create an org](#sign-up-and-create-an-org)\\n\\n### [](#building-bitbucket-cloud-projects)Building Bitbucket Cloud projects?\\n\\nIf you want to primarily build **Bitbucket Cloud** projects, sign up with your Bitbucket account. After you connect your Bitbucket account you can go ahead and start setting up projects, following the [Create a project in CircleCI](../create-project/#set-up-a-project) page. Your org in CircleCI maps directly to your Bitbucket workspace.\\n\\n1. Select **Sign Up With Bitbucket**.\\n2. Log in to your Bitbucket Cloud account\\n3. Enter your email address and create a password.\\n4. Choose between creating an organization or joining your teammates in an existing org:  \\n   * If you are **creating a new CircleCI org** for your team, follow the [Create an org](#sign-up-and-create-an-org) steps below.  \\n   * If you want to join an existing shared organization, follow the steps on the [Join your teammates](../invite-your-team/) page.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up-and-create-an-org)Create an org\\n\\nThis section covers creating a new CircleCI organization. Once your organization is created, you can start building right away or invite your teammates to join.\\n\\n1. From your user homepage select **Get Started** in the \\\"Start a new organization\\\" card.\\n2. Give your organization a name and select **Let’s Go**.\\n3. Create a project to get started. Select **Get Started** in the \\\"Create a project\\\" card, and refer to the [Create a project](../create-project/#create-a-project) page for further steps.  \\nIf you choose not to connect your code at this time, you can still explore some example projects within the CircleCI app.  \\n   * You can take a look at a popular open source project building on CircleCI, [React by Facebook](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/facebook/react).  \\n   * Or some of our own sample projects: a [sample JavaScript app](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/CircleCI-Public/sample-javascript-cfd/), and a [sample Python app](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/CircleCI-Public/sample-python-cfd/).\\n4. Invite your team by navigating to **Organization Settings** **People**. For more information on inviting people to your org, see the [Join your teammates](../invite-your-team/#invite-teammates) page.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up-with-an-invite)Sign up with an invite\\n\\nIf you are signing up using an invite from your team, you will be _joining_ an existing CircleCI organization.\\n\\n1. To create an account for free with CircleCI, follow the link in your invite email and enter your email address and create a password.\\n2. On the next screen, respond to prompts that best describe your role and your engineering organization.\\n3. Finally, you will be taken to your organization’s pipelines dashboard in the CircleCI web app. From here, you can follow projects or trigger pipelines in projects to which you have access in your organization, or [create new projects](../create-project/).\\n\\n| |  If you are setting up a project for the first time, you may need to authenticate with your VCS provider. Once you have completed a one-time authentication, you will be able to set up subsequent projects in CircleCI more quickly. Refer to the [Creating a Project](../create-project/) guide for more information. |\\n| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\n\\nFor a guide to integrating your code with CircleCI, see the [Users, organizations, and integrations guide](../../permissions-authentication/users-organizations-and-integrations-guide/).\\n\\n## [](#terms)Terms\\n\\nBy signing up, you are agreeing to our [SaaS Agreement](https://circleci.com/terms-of-service/) and [Privacy Policy](https://circleci.com/privacy/). We ask for read/write access to make your experience seamless on CircleCI. If you are a GitHub user and aren’t ready to share access to your private projects, you can choose public repositories instead. Protected by reCAPTCHA, Google [Privacy Policy](https://policies.google.com/privacy?hl=en) and [Terms of Service](https://policies.google.com/terms?hl=en) apply.\\n\\n## [](#next-steps)Next steps\\n\\n* Read the [Join your teammates](../invite-your-team/) page for steps on adding team members to your CircleCI org.\\n* Go to the [Hello World](../hello-world/) page to learn the basics of setting up a CircleCI configuration file.\\n* Read the [Concepts](../../about-circleci/concepts/) page for an overview of foundational CircleCI concepts such as pipelines, executors.\\n* Refer to the full [CircleCI configuration reference](../../../reference/configuration-reference/) for creating a `.circleci/config.yml` file, which determines your execution environment and automates your tests.\\n\\n####  Suggest an edit to this page\\n\\n* [Make a contribution](https://github.com/circleci/circleci-docs/edit/main/docs/guides/modules/getting-started/pages/first-steps.adoc)\\n* [Learn how to contribute](https://github.com/circleci/circleci-docs/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)\\n\\n####  Still need help?\\n\\n* [Ask the CircleCI community](https://discuss.circleci.com/)\\n* [Join the research community](https://circleci.com/research/)\\n* [Visit our support site](https://support.circleci.com/hc/en-us/)\\n\\n![CircleCI Logo](../../../_/img/logo.svg) ![CircleCI Logo](../../../_/img/logo-dark.svg) \\n\\n* © CircleCI, Inc. Services](../../../services/services-overview/)\\n* [ Contributors](../../../contributors/docs-style/)\\n\\n* [Home](../../../)\\n* [Guides](./)\\n* Getting started\\n* First steps\\n\\n# Sign up and try CircleCI\\n\\n[  2 months ago](https://github.com/circleci/circleci-docs/commit/0c04b7910fb0d21748929dedff736403016b6903) \\n\\nCloud \\n\\nMarkdown \\n* [ View markdown](index.md)\\n* Copy markdown\\n\\nTo start building on CircleCI, go to the [Sign Up](https://circleci.com/signup/) page or follow an email invite link from your team, and create an account. After signing up you can create an organization or find your teammates.\\n\\nCircleCI supports GitHub, Bitbucket, and GitLab projects.\\n\\nIf you use **GitHub**, **GitLab**, or **Bitbucket Data Center** and are creating a CircleCI organization for your team, you will set the version control system (VCS) integration type, and authenticate with your VCS provider during the signup process. Team members that you invite to your CircleCI org can join without authenticating with your VCS. However, if they are to create new projects, they will authenticate during the project creation process.\\n\\nIf you use **Bitbucket Cloud**, every team member signing up for a new account and joining your org will authenticate with Bitbucket _during_ the signup process.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up)Sign up\\n\\nFrom the [Sign Up](https://circleci.com/signup/) page, do one of the following:\\n\\n### [](#github-gitlab-bitbucket-data-center)Building GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket Data Center projects?\\n\\nIf you want to build mainly **GitHub**, **GitLab** or **Bitbucket Data Center** projects, sign up with your email address to create an account.\\n\\n| |  If you want to collaborate on an existing org you can create an account and request an invite later. |\\n| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\n\\n1. Select **Sign Up**.\\n2. Enter your email address and create a password.\\n3. Answer a few questions about how you plan to use CircleCI.\\n4. You can now choose one of the following:  \\n   * Use the [Find Your Team](../invite-your-team/) option to connect your account to a shared org  \\n   * [Create an org](#sign-up-and-create-an-org)\\n\\n### [](#building-bitbucket-cloud-projects)Building Bitbucket Cloud projects?\\n\\nIf you want to primarily build **Bitbucket Cloud** projects, sign up with your Bitbucket account. After you connect your Bitbucket account you can go ahead and start setting up projects, following the [Create a project in CircleCI](../create-project/#set-up-a-project) page. Your org in CircleCI maps directly to your Bitbucket workspace.\\n\\n1. Select **Sign Up With Bitbucket**.\\n2. Log in to your Bitbucket Cloud account\\n3. Enter your email address and create a password.\\n4. Choose between creating an organization or joining your teammates in an existing org:  \\n   * If you are **creating a new CircleCI org** for your team, follow the [Create an org](#sign-up-and-create-an-org) steps below.  \\n   * If you want to join an existing shared organization, follow the steps on the [Join your teammates](../invite-your-team/) page.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up-and-create-an-org)Create an org\\n\\nThis section covers creating a new CircleCI organization. Once your organization is created, you can start building right away or invite your teammates to join.\\n\\n1. From your user homepage select **Get Started** in the \\\"Start a new organization\\\" card.\\n2. Give your organization a name and select **Let’s Go**.\\n3. Create a project to get started. Select **Get Started** in the \\\"Create a project\\\" card, and refer to the [Create a project](../create-project/#create-a-project) page for further steps.  \\nIf you choose not to connect your code at this time, you can still explore some example projects within the CircleCI app.  \\n   * You can take a look at a popular open source project building on CircleCI, [React by Facebook](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/facebook/react).  \\n   * Or some of our own sample projects: a [sample JavaScript app](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/CircleCI-Public/sample-javascript-cfd/), and a [sample Python app](https://app.circleci.com/pipelines/github/CircleCI-Public/sample-python-cfd/).\\n4. Invite your team by navigating to **Organization Settings** **People**. For more information on inviting people to your org, see the [Join your teammates](../invite-your-team/#invite-teammates) page.\\n\\n## [](#sign-up-with-an-invite)Sign up with an invite\\n\\nIf you are signing up using an invite from your team, you will be _joining_ an existing CircleCI organization.\\n\\n1. To create an account for free with CircleCI, follow the link in your invite email and enter your email address and create a password.\\n2. On the next screen, respond to prompts that best describe your role and your engineering organization.\\n3. Finally, you will be taken to your organization’s pipelines dashboard in the CircleCI web app. From here, you can follow projects or trigger pipelines in projects to which you have access in your organization, or [create new projects](../create-project/).\\n\\n| |  If you are setting up a project for the first time, you may need to authenticate with your VCS provider. Once you have completed a one-time authentication, you will be able to set up subsequent projects in CircleCI more quickly. Refer to the [Creating a Project](../create-project/) guide for more information. |\\n| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\n\\nFor a guide to integrating your code with CircleCI, see the [Users, organizations, and integrations guide](../../permissions-authentication/users-organizations-and-integrations-guide/).\\n\\n## [](#terms)Terms\\n\\nBy signing up, you are agreeing to our [SaaS Agreement](https://circleci.com/terms-of-service/) and [Privacy Policy](https://circleci.com/privacy/). We ask for read/write access to make your experience seamless on CircleCI. If you are a GitHub user and aren’t ready to share access to your private projects, you can choose public repositories instead. Covers `harness init`, harness-managed CI migration, governance checks, and Codex environment action-sync guidance. Do not use for unrelated coding, general deployment, or broad cloud work.\nmetadata:\n  skill-type: team_automation\n---\n\n# Coding Harness\nOperate `@brainwav/coding-harness` with command-accurate setup flows, conservative mutation rules, and explicit governance boundaries.\n\n## Table of Contents\n- [When to use](#when-to-use)\n- [Required inputs](#required-inputs)\n- [Deliverables](#deliverables)\n- [Philosophy](#philosophy)\n- [Workflow](#workflow)\n- [Validation](#validation)\n- [References](#references)\n- [Constraints and safety](#constraints-and-safety)\n- [Anti-patterns](#anti-patterns)\n- [Failure mode](#failure-mode)\n- [Gotchas](#gotchas)\n- [Examples](#examples)\n- [See Also](#see-also)\n\n## When to use\nUse this skill when the request is to:\n- install `@brainwav/coding-harness` in a repository and verify the scaffold safely;\n- bootstrap or repair `harness init`, including preview, update, migrate, and rollback paths;\n- explain what coding-harness can and cannot do, including required tokens, remote-check boundaries, and environment setup;\n- audit whether `.codex/environments/environment.toml` action blocks are aligned with current project scripts;\n- guide or execute GitHub Actions to CircleCI cutover using `harness ci-migrate` instead of manual workflow deletion.\n\nDo not use this skill for:\n- unrelated application feature work;\n- generic cloud deployment work that is not about harness governance;\n- broad security reviews that are not tied to coding-harness install, policy, or verification surfaces.\n- ordinary questions or creative tasks that do not involve coding-harness. In those cases, answer normally or route to a more relevant skill.\n\n## Required inputs\n- target repository path and package-manager context;\n- current harness state: `not installed`, `installed`, `needs update`, or `broken`;\n- execution posture: `no-execution` guidance or command-running execution mode;\n- auth posture for remote checks, such as PAT or GitHub App JWT, when `verify-greptile` or similar remote checks are requested;\n- desired verification depth: lightweight setup confirmation or deep runtime/artifact validation.\n\nIf details are missing, make the safest reasonable assumption:\n- default to no-execution mode for explanation-only asks;\n- default to preview-first commands before mutative operations;\n- default to skipping remote checks until the user provides auth material.\n\n## Deliverables\nProduce only what the request needs, usually:\n- a setup or remediation summary with exact commands run;\n- a file-level change summary when harness scaffolds or updates repository files;\n- a validation ladder summary that distinguishes baseline gates, deep gates, and conditional harness-specific checks;\n- a capability boundary summary covering `can do`, `cannot do`, and `requires user-provided auth`;\n- explicit pass/fail evidence plus the next safe action when anything remains unresolved;\n- machine-readable reports that preserve `schema_version` whenever the output is schema-bound.\n\n## Philosophy\n- Prefer deterministic, evidence-backed setup over assumptions.\n- Keep installation and update guidance reversible, preview-first, and command-accurate.\n- Treat capability boundaries as part of the contract, especially around auth, destructive changes, and enforced scaffold files.\n- Separate `skill bundle is accurate` from `target repo is fully green` because those are different claims.\n- When refusing unsafe or unsupported requests, say `cannot` plainly and explain the missing evidence or prerequisite.\n- Start with the smallest viable harness boundary, keep scope tight, and expand only when the user clearly asks for more than bootstrap, CI migration, environment action-sync, or auth-bound verification.\n\n## Workflow\n1. Confirm execution mode.\n- Do stay in no-execution mode when the user wants explanation or planning because that keeps guidance reversible.\n- Do switch to execution mode only when command evidence is required because completion claims must be grounded in the live repo state.\n- Do not inspect the current workspace when the request is explanation-only and no target repo path was provided because the answer should come from the documented harness contract first.\n\n2. Preflight the repository.\n- Do confirm repo root, toolchain availability, and current harness state because path-sensitive or multi-step work is fragile without preflight.\n- Do run `source scripts/codex-preflight.sh && preflight_repo` when available because harness-enabled repos often encode extra local policy there.\n\n3. Install or upgrade harness conservatively.\n- Do prefer `mise install -g npm:@brainwav/coding-harness` for consumer repos because it matches the current recommended global install posture.\n- Do bootstrap with `harness init --dry-run` followed by `harness init` because the current CLI-safe path is preview-first.\n- Do not pass `--ci circleci` to `harness init` because the current CLI treats the extra positional token as a target directory in this flow.\n- Do use `harness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --dry-run`, then `--apply`, `verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>`, `commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>`, and `abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>` because cutover must stay reversible and snapshot-bound.\n- Do use project-local source execution only when you are actively developing the coding-harness repository because consumer repos should follow the packaged CLI contract.\n\n4. Validate setup and policy state.\n- Do run the baseline repository gate first because `pnpm check` catches the broad integration surface expected by the tool.\n- Do escalate to `pnpm test:deep` when runtime behavior or artifact formats changed because baseline checks are not enough for behavior-affecting work.\n- Do use targeted harness checks such as `harness check-environment`, `verify-greptile`, `check-authz`, `docs-gate`, and `tooling-audit` when the user asks for specific governance evidence.\n- Do rely on structured `--json` output for gates and `health --auto-fix --dry-run` because machine-readable findings are safer to automate than table scraping.\n- Do answer directly that a repo is not verified yet when checks were skipped because unsupported certainty is worse than an incomplete status.\n\n5. Report capability boundaries explicitly.\n- Do say which checks were actually run because remote verification and auth-dependent gates are optional until credentials exist.\n- Do call out user-managed surfaces such as secrets, tokens, branch protection ownership, and non-autogenerated environment files because harness does not replace human authority there.\n\n6. Record repo-specific learnings when applicable.\n- Do read `~/.codex/instructions/Learnings.md` first and `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` second because the skill uses both global and repo-scoped memory.\n- Do append only repo-specific lessons to `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` when `.harness/` exists because harness repos treat that file as an append-only local knowledge base.\n\n## Validation\nRun the smallest relevant checks first, then broaden before claiming completion:\n\n```bash\nharness --help\nharness init --dry-run\npnpm check\npnpm test:deep\n```\n\nUse targeted governance or lifecycle commands as needed:\n\n```bash\nharness init --check-updates\nharness init --update\nharness init --migrate\nharness init --rollback\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --dry-run\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --apply\nharness ci-migrate verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness ci-migrate commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness ci-migrate abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness verify-greptile\nharness verify-greptile --token <token-or-jwt> --owner <owner> --repo <repo>\nharness check-authz --contract <path> --repo <owner/repo> --branch <branch>\nharness check-environment --contract <path> --attestation <path>\nharness docs-gate --mode advisory --json\nharness tooling-audit --path <dir> --format table\nharness evidence-verify --files <paths>\nharness health --auto-fix --dry-run --json\n```\n\nCanonical skill maintenance gates after edits:\n\n```bash\nbash scripts/lint_openai_skill_format.sh --mode strict\nbash scripts/lint_progressive_disclosure.sh --mode warn\npython3 scripts/gotcha_pipeline.py validate\npython3 scripts/check-see-also.py . --changed-files utilities/coding-harness/SKILL.md\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/validate_skill_graph_profiles.py --repo-root . --expected-count 0\nbash scripts/sync_skills_sandbox_safe.sh\nbash scripts/lint_skill_types.sh\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/skill_gate.py utilities/coding-harness --require-fail-fast --require-security-evals\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/run_skill_evals.py utilities/coding-harness --eval-mode release --runner codex --capture-jsonl --timeout-profile codex-heavy --profile d --codex-fallback-profile ''\n```\n\nFail fast on the first blocking gate, fix the specific issue, rerun that gate, and only then continue to broader validation.\n\n## References\n- `references/agent-install-guide.md`\n  Read when: you need the human-readable install, update, and CI migration sequence with rollback.\n- `references/agent-install.json`\n  Read when: you need machine-readable phases, commands, or scaffolded file lists.\n- `references/setup-and-commands.md`\n  Read when: you need the wider command map, lifecycle commands, or CI secret wiring details.\n- `references/structured-json-output.md`\n  Read when: you need the `GateResult` schema, `jq` extraction patterns, or `health --auto-fix` usage.\n- `references/contract.yaml`\n  Read when: you need the machine-checkable input/output contract for this skill.\n- `references/evals.yaml`\n  Read when: you need trigger coverage, adversarial cases, or release-eval inputs.\n- `references/plan.md`\n  Read when: you need preserved implementation context from the packaged-skill work rather than the operator-facing wrapper alone.\n- `agents/openai.yaml`\n  Read when: you need the display metadata used by external skill packaging surfaces.\n\n## Constraints and safety\n- Redact secrets, tokens, and sensitive repository data in logs, artifacts, and summaries.\n- Require explicit user confirmation before destructive or high-impact mutations.\n- Treat `harness --help` as command truth when older docs disagree.\n- Do not claim full setup success without command evidence from the current repository state.\n- Treat remote checks as optional until the user provides the required auth material.\n- Do not hand-edit enforced harness outputs such as `harness.contract.json`, `.circleci/config.yml`, or `scripts/check-environment.sh` because `harness init --update` will overwrite them.\n- Only auto-update `.codex/environments/environment.toml` when the file is harness-generated because user-owned environment config needs an approval checkpoint.\n\n## Anti-patterns\n- claiming harness is configured without running verification commands;\n- deleting `.github/workflows/` files manually instead of using `harness ci-migrate`;\n- treating `pnpm check` and `pnpm test:deep` as interchangeable when runtime behavior changed;\n- reporting remote verification as passed when auth was never supplied;\n- applying `harness init --update` without previewing first when repo state is unclear;\n- editing enforced scaffold files by hand and assuming the changes will persist.\n\n## Failure mode\n- If the target repo path cannot be verified, stop before mutating anything and ask for the exact root.\n- If auth-dependent checks are requested without credentials, explain the missing prerequisite and continue only with local/non-remote validation.\n- If command help output conflicts with packaged docs, follow the command output, call out the drift, and repair the canonical copy before claiming completion.\n- If the repo is already red on unrelated gates, separate `skill bundle is correct` from `repo aggregate is green` instead of overstating success.\n- If the request is unrelated to coding-harness, do not force this workflow onto it; either answer normally or route to the correct skill.\n\n## Gotchas\n- Symptom: `harness init --ci circleci` fails with a path-resolution error. Cause: the current CLI interprets `circleci` like a target directory in that flow. Do instead: run `harness init --dry-run` and then `harness init`. Check: rerun `harness init --dry-run` and confirm the scaffold plan renders normally.\n- Symptom: CI migration guidance looks right but rollback is missing. Cause: older summaries often stop after `commit`. Do instead: preserve the full snapshot-based sequence including `abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>`. Check: confirm preview, apply, verify, commit, and abort all appear in both `SKILL.md` and `references/agent-install-guide.md`.\n- Symptom: an agent claims repo setup is complete after only local checks. Cause: remote gates such as Greptile verification were skipped because auth was missing. Do instead: report the local pass separately from auth-blocked remote checks. Check: the final summary should say exactly which checks were skipped and why.\n- Symptom: `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` guidance is applied to a repo without `.harness/`. Cause: the memory layer is repo-specific, not universal. Do instead: skip creation when `.harness/` is absent. Check: only read or append the repo memory file when the harness directory exists.\n- Symptom: `run_skill_evals.py` fails early with `Unsupported parameter: 'reasoning.summary'` under a Spark-oriented Codex profile. Cause: the active profile/model does not support the runner's default reasoning setting. Do instead: pin the eval run to `--profile d --codex-fallback-profile ''`. Check: the release run should emit normal case artifacts and a populated `scorecard.json`.\n\n## Examples\n- When the user asks: \"Can you help me add the Brainwav harness to this repo and tell me which steps still need my credentials?\"\n- When the user asks: \"Please map the safe bootstrap flow for this repo, including preview, validation, update, and rollback commands.\"\n- When the user asks: \"Help me inspect whether our Codex environment actions are still aligned with the current scripts.\"\n- When the user asks: \"Can you explain whether we should use a GitHub App JWT or a PAT for the harness verification checks?\"\n- Do not trigger for: \"Build a new dashboard for our admin panel.\"\n\n## See Also\n| Skill | When to use |\n|---|---|\n| [[bootstrap]] | Clone and bootstrap a repository before coding-harness setup begins |\n| [[circleci]] | Work on CircleCI-specific pipeline design or migration details beyond the harness wrapper |\n| [[verification-before-completion]] | Add a stronger final verification pass before claiming a repo change is complete |\n| [[systematic-debugging]] | Root-cause failing harness commands or broken repo state before applying fixes |\n\n**Topic map:** [[agent-ops]]"}],"structured_content":{"content":"---\nname: coding-harness\ndescription: Use when a repository needs `@brainwav/coding-harness` installed, bootstrapped, updated, audited, or explained. Covers `harness init`, harness-managed CI migration, governance checks, and Codex environment action-sync guidance. Do not use for unrelated coding, general deployment, or broad cloud work.\nmetadata:\n  skill-type: team_automation\n---\n\n# Coding Harness\nOperate `@brainwav/coding-harness` with command-accurate setup flows, conservative mutation rules, and explicit governance boundaries.\n\n## Table of Contents\n- [When to use](#when-to-use)\n- [Required inputs](#required-inputs)\n- [Deliverables](#deliverables)\n- [Philosophy](#philosophy)\n- [Workflow](#workflow)\n- [Validation](#validation)\n- [References](#references)\n- [Constraints and safety](#constraints-and-safety)\n- [Anti-patterns](#anti-patterns)\n- [Failure mode](#failure-mode)\n- [Gotchas](#gotchas)\n- [Examples](#examples)\n- [See Also](#see-also)\n\n## When to use\nUse this skill when the request is to:\n- install `@brainwav/coding-harness` in a repository and verify the scaffold safely;\n- bootstrap or repair `harness init`, including preview, update, migrate, and rollback paths;\n- explain what coding-harness can and cannot do, including required tokens, remote-check boundaries, and environment setup;\n- audit whether `.codex/environments/environment.toml` action blocks are aligned with current project scripts;\n- guide or execute GitHub Actions to CircleCI cutover using `harness ci-migrate` instead of manual workflow deletion.\n\nDo not use this skill for:\n- unrelated application feature work;\n- generic cloud deployment work that is not about harness governance;\n- broad security reviews that are not tied to coding-harness install, policy, or verification surfaces.\n- ordinary questions or creative tasks that do not involve coding-harness. In those cases, answer normally or route to a more relevant skill.\n\n## Required inputs\n- target repository path and package-manager context;\n- current harness state: `not installed`, `installed`, `needs update`, or `broken`;\n- execution posture: `no-execution` guidance or command-running execution mode;\n- auth posture for remote checks, such as PAT or GitHub App JWT, when `verify-greptile` or similar remote checks are requested;\n- desired verification depth: lightweight setup confirmation or deep runtime/artifact validation.\n\nIf details are missing, make the safest reasonable assumption:\n- default to no-execution mode for explanation-only asks;\n- default to preview-first commands before mutative operations;\n- default to skipping remote checks until the user provides auth material.\n\n## Deliverables\nProduce only what the request needs, usually:\n- a setup or remediation summary with exact commands run;\n- a file-level change summary when harness scaffolds or updates repository files;\n- a validation ladder summary that distinguishes baseline gates, deep gates, and conditional harness-specific checks;\n- a capability boundary summary covering `can do`, `cannot do`, and `requires user-provided auth`;\n- explicit pass/fail evidence plus the next safe action when anything remains unresolved;\n- machine-readable reports that preserve `schema_version` whenever the output is schema-bound.\n\n## Philosophy\n- Prefer deterministic, evidence-backed setup over assumptions.\n- Keep installation and update guidance reversible, preview-first, and command-accurate.\n- Treat capability boundaries as part of the contract, especially around auth, destructive changes, and enforced scaffold files.\n- Separate `skill bundle is accurate` from `target repo is fully green` because those are different claims.\n- When refusing unsafe or unsupported requests, say `cannot` plainly and explain the missing evidence or prerequisite.\n- Start with the smallest viable harness boundary, keep scope tight, and expand only when the user clearly asks for more than bootstrap, CI migration, environment action-sync, or auth-bound verification.\n\n## Workflow\n1. Confirm execution mode.\n- Do stay in no-execution mode when the user wants explanation or planning because that keeps guidance reversible.\n- Do switch to execution mode only when command evidence is required because completion claims must be grounded in the live repo state.\n- Do not inspect the current workspace when the request is explanation-only and no target repo path was provided because the answer should come from the documented harness contract first.\n\n2. Preflight the repository.\n- Do confirm repo root, toolchain availability, and current harness state because path-sensitive or multi-step work is fragile without preflight.\n- Do run `source scripts/codex-preflight.sh && preflight_repo` when available because harness-enabled repos often encode extra local policy there.\n\n3. Install or upgrade harness conservatively.\n- Do prefer `mise install -g npm:@brainwav/coding-harness` for consumer repos because it matches the current recommended global install posture.\n- Do bootstrap with `harness init --dry-run` followed by `harness init` because the current CLI-safe path is preview-first.\n- Do not pass `--ci circleci` to `harness init` because the current CLI treats the extra positional token as a target directory in this flow.\n- Do use `harness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --dry-run`, then `--apply`, `verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>`, `commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>`, and `abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>` because cutover must stay reversible and snapshot-bound.\n- Do use project-local source execution only when you are actively developing the coding-harness repository because consumer repos should follow the packaged CLI contract.\n\n4. Validate setup and policy state.\n- Do run the baseline repository gate first because `pnpm check` catches the broad integration surface expected by the tool.\n- Do escalate to `pnpm test:deep` when runtime behavior or artifact formats changed because baseline checks are not enough for behavior-affecting work.\n- Do use targeted harness checks such as `harness check-environment`, `verify-greptile`, `check-authz`, `docs-gate`, and `tooling-audit` when the user asks for specific governance evidence.\n- Do rely on structured `--json` output for gates and `health --auto-fix --dry-run` because machine-readable findings are safer to automate than table scraping.\n- Do answer directly that a repo is not verified yet when checks were skipped because unsupported certainty is worse than an incomplete status.\n\n5. Report capability boundaries explicitly.\n- Do say which checks were actually run because remote verification and auth-dependent gates are optional until credentials exist.\n- Do call out user-managed surfaces such as secrets, tokens, branch protection ownership, and non-autogenerated environment files because harness does not replace human authority there.\n\n6. Record repo-specific learnings when applicable.\n- Do read `~/.codex/instructions/Learnings.md` first and `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` second because the skill uses both global and repo-scoped memory.\n- Do append only repo-specific lessons to `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` when `.harness/` exists because harness repos treat that file as an append-only local knowledge base.\n\n## Validation\nRun the smallest relevant checks first, then broaden before claiming completion:\n\n```bash\nharness --help\nharness init --dry-run\npnpm check\npnpm test:deep\n```\n\nUse targeted governance or lifecycle commands as needed:\n\n```bash\nharness init --check-updates\nharness init --update\nharness init --migrate\nharness init --rollback\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --dry-run\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --apply\nharness ci-migrate verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness ci-migrate commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness ci-migrate abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>\nharness verify-greptile\nharness verify-greptile --token <token-or-jwt> --owner <owner> --repo <repo>\nharness check-authz --contract <path> --repo <owner/repo> --branch <branch>\nharness check-environment --contract <path> --attestation <path>\nharness docs-gate --mode advisory --json\nharness tooling-audit --path <dir> --format table\nharness evidence-verify --files <paths>\nharness health --auto-fix --dry-run --json\n```\n\nCanonical skill maintenance gates after edits:\n\n```bash\nbash scripts/lint_openai_skill_format.sh --mode strict\nbash scripts/lint_progressive_disclosure.sh --mode warn\npython3 scripts/gotcha_pipeline.py validate\npython3 scripts/check-see-also.py . --changed-files utilities/coding-harness/SKILL.md\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/validate_skill_graph_profiles.py --repo-root . --expected-count 0\nbash scripts/sync_skills_sandbox_safe.sh\nbash scripts/lint_skill_types.sh\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/skill_gate.py utilities/coding-harness --require-fail-fast --require-security-evals\npython3 utilities/skill-builder/scripts/run_skill_evals.py utilities/coding-harness --eval-mode release --runner codex --capture-jsonl --timeout-profile codex-heavy --profile d --codex-fallback-profile ''\n```\n\nFail fast on the first blocking gate, fix the specific issue, rerun that gate, and only then continue to broader validation.\n\n## References\n- `references/agent-install-guide.md`\n  Read when: you need the human-readable install, update, and CI migration sequence with rollback.\n- `references/agent-install.json`\n  Read when: you need machine-readable phases, commands, or scaffolded skill.\n- `references/evals.yaml`\n  Read when: you need trigger coverage, adversarial cases, or release-eval inputs.\n- `references/plan.md`\n  Read when: you need preserved implementation context from the packaged-skill work rather than the operator-facing wrapper alone.\n- `agents/openai.yaml`\n  Read when: you need the display metadata used by external skill packaging surfaces.\n\n## Constraints and safety\n- Redact secrets, tokens, and sensitive repository data in logs, artifacts, and summaries.\n- Require explicit user confirmation before destructive or high-impact mutations.\n- Treat `harness --help` as command truth when older docs disagree.\n- Do not claim full setup success without command evidence from the current repository state.\n- Treat remote checks as optional until the user provides the required auth material.\n- Do not hand-edit enforced harness outputs such as `harness.contract.json`, `.circleci/config.yml`, or `scripts/check-environment.sh` because `harness init --update` will overwrite them.\n- Only auto-update `.codex/environments/environment.toml` when the file is harness-generated because user-owned environment config needs an approval checkpoint.\n\n## Anti-patterns\n- claiming harness is configured without running verification commands;\n- deleting `.github/workflows/` files manually instead of using `harness ci-migrate`;\n- treating `pnpm check` and `pnpm test:deep` as interchangeable when runtime behavior changed;\n- reporting remote verification as passed when auth was never supplied;\n- applying `harness init --update` without previewing first when repo state is unclear;\n- editing enforced scaffold files by hand and assuming the changes will persist.\n\n## Failure mode\n- If the target repo path cannot be verified, stop before mutating anything and ask for the exact root.\n- If auth-dependent checks are requested without credentials, explain the missing prerequisite and continue only with local/non-remote validation.\n- If command help output conflicts with packaged docs, follow the command output, call out the drift, and repair the canonical copy before claiming completion.\n- If the repo is already red on unrelated gates, separate `skill bundle is correct` from `repo aggregate is green` instead of overstating success.\n- If the request is unrelated to coding-harness, do not force this workflow onto it; either answer normally or route to the correct skill.\n\n## Gotchas\n- Symptom: `harness init --ci circleci` fails with a path-resolution error. Cause: the current CLI interprets `circleci` like a target directory in that flow. Do instead: run `harness init --dry-run` and then `harness init`. Check: rerun `harness init --dry-run` and confirm the scaffold plan renders normally.\n- Symptom: CI migration guidance looks right but rollback is missing. Cause: older summaries often stop after `commit`. Do instead: preserve the full snapshot-based sequence including `abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>`. Check: confirm preview, apply, verify, commit, and abort all appear in both `SKILL.md` and `references/agent-install-guide.md`.\n- Symptom: an agent claims repo setup is complete after only local checks. Cause: remote gates such as Greptile verification were skipped because auth was missing. Do instead: report the local pass separately from auth-blocked remote checks. Check: the final summary should say exactly which checks were skipped and why.\n- Symptom: `.harness/memory/LEARNINGS.md` guidance is applied to a repo without `.harness/`. Cause: the memory layer is repo-specific, not universal. Do instead: skip creation when `.harness/` is absent. Check: only read or append the repo memory file when the harness directory exists.\n- Symptom: `run_skill_evals.py` fails early with `Unsupported parameter: 'reasoning.summary'` under a Spark-oriented Codex profile. Cause: the active profile/model does not support the runner's default reasoning setting. Do instead: pin the eval run to `--profile d --codex-fallback-profile ''`. Check: the release run should emit normal case artifacts and a populated `scorecard.json`.\n\n## Examples\n- When the user asks: \"Can you help me add the Brainwav harness to this repo and tell me which steps still need my credentials?\"\n- When the user asks: \"Please map the safe bootstrap flow for this repo, including preview, validation, update, and rollback commands.\"\n- When the user asks: \"Help me inspect whether our Codex environment actions are still aligned with the current scripts.\"\n- When the user asks: \"Can you explain whether we should use a GitHub App JWT or a PAT for the harness verification checks?\"\n- Do not trigger for: \"Build a new dashboard for our admin panel.\"\n\n## See Also\n| Skill | When to use |\n|---|---|\n| [[bootstrap]] | Clone and bootstrap a repository before coding-harness setup begins |\n| [[circleci]] | Work on CircleCI-specific pipeline design or migration details beyond the harness wrapper |\n| [[verification-before-completion]] | Add a stronger final verification pass before claiming a repo change is complete |\n| [[systematic-debugging]] | Root-cause failing harness commands or broken repo state before applying fixes |\n\n**Topic map:** [[agent-ops]]"}},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}

Coding Harness setup and command reference\n\n## Table of Contents\n- [Prerequisites](#prerequisites)\n- [Command truth source](#command-truth-source)\n- [Install modes](#install-modes)\n- [Bootstrap workflow](#bootstrap-workflow)\n- [Abbreviations](#abbreviations)\n- [Metadata](#metadata)\n- [Invariants](#invariants)\n- [States](#states)\n- [Transition Table (Canonical)](#transition-table-canonical)\n- [Update workflow for existing repos](#update-workflow-for-existing-repos)\n- [Error Handling](#error-handling)\n- [Idempotency](#idempotency)\n- [Execution Modes](#execution-modes)\n- [Dry-Run Simulation](#dry-run-simulation)\n- [Observability Logs](#observability-logs)\n- [Validation Checklist](#validation-checklist)\n- [Executor pseudocode](#executor-pseudocode)\n- [Validation ladder](#validation-ladder)\n- [environment.toml action sync behavior](#environmenttoml-action-sync-behavior)\n- [Command map](#command-map)\n- [Capability boundaries](#capability-boundaries)\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Node.js `>=24.0.0`\n- `pnpm` (repo package manager)\n- Repository write access for `harness init` template changes\n- For remote GitHub checks, a valid token (GitHub App JWT is preferred when verifying App installation state)\n\n## Command truth source\n\nPrefer runtime command help over stale prose snapshots:\n\n```bash\nharness --help\n```\n\nIf harness is not installed globally in the current shell, use:\n\n```bash\npnpm exec tsx src/cli.ts --help\n```\n\n## Install modes\n\n### Global npm install (required for generated preflight)\n\n```bash\nnpm install -g @brainwav/coding-harness\nharness --help\n```\n\nRecommended managed install:\n\n```bash\nmise install -g npm:@brainwav/coding-harness\n```\n\nRequired private package auth wiring:\n\n```bash\nexport NPM_TOKEN=<token>\n# CircleCI project settings -> Environment Variables:\n#   NPM_TOKEN\n```\n\n### Project-local source CLI (only for coding-harness development)\n\nUse this only when developing inside the `coding-harness` source repository itself.\nGenerated `scripts/check-environment.sh` in consumer repositories does not fall back\nto `pnpm exec tsx src/cli.ts ...`; it requires the global `harness` binary from npm.\n\n## Bootstrap workflow\n\n1. Install dependencies:\n\n## Abbreviations\n| Abbr | Meaning |\n| --- | --- |\n| `S` | state |\n| `E` | event |\n| `G` | guard |\n| `A` | action |\n| `N` | next state |\n\n## Metadata\n| Field | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| `owner` | `TODO` |\n| `max_duration` | `TODO` |\n| `escalation` | `TODO` |\n\n## Invariants\n- TODO invariant 1\n- TODO invariant 2\n\n## States\n```txt\nS0 (non-terminal)\nDONE (terminal)\nFAIL (terminal)\nBLOCKED (terminal)\n```\n\n## Transition Table (Canonical)\n`S | E | G | A | N`\n\n### State machine\n\n```txt\nB0 PREFLIGHT -> B1 DEPS -> B2 INIT_PREVIEW -> B3 INIT_APPLY -> B4 VALIDATE -> B5 READY\n     |             |            |              |\n     +-----------> BX FAIL <----+--------------+\n```\n\n### Transition table (`S | E | G | A | N`)\n\n| S | E | G | A | N |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `B0 PREFLIGHT` | `preflight_ok` | preflight script passes | `source scripts/codex-preflight.sh && preflight_repo` | `B1 DEPS` |\n| `B1 DEPS` | `deps_installed` | `pnpm` available | `pnpm install` | `B2 INIT_PREVIEW` |\n| `B2 INIT_PREVIEW` | `preview_ok` | dry-run exits clean | `harness init --dry-run` | `B3 INIT_APPLY` |\n| `B3 INIT_APPLY` | `init_applied` | templates generated | `harness init` | `B4 VALIDATE` |\n| `B4 VALIDATE` | `checks_pass` | baseline gate passes | `pnpm check` and optional `harness verify-greptile` | `B5 READY` |\n| `B*` | `error` | any guard fails | capture blocker + stop | `BX FAIL` |\n\nExpected scaffold lane in `B3`:\n- `.greptile/config.json`\n- `.greptile/rules.md`\n- `.greptile/files.json`\n- `.github/workflows/greptile-review.yml`\n\n## Update workflow for existing repos\n\n### State machine\n\n```txt\nU0 CHECK -> U1 PREVIEW -> U2 APPLY -> U3 VALIDATE -> U4 DONE\n   |          |           |             |\n   +--------> UX FAIL <---+-------------+\n```\n\n### Transition table (`S | E | G | A | N`)\n\n| S | E | G | A | N |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `U0 CHECK` | `updates_found` | update check runs | `harness init --check-updates` | `U1 PREVIEW` |\n| `U1 PREVIEW` | `preview_ok` | dry-run output reviewed | `harness init --dry-run --update` | `U2 APPLY` |\n| `U2 APPLY` | `apply_ok` | selected update strategy confirmed | `harness init --update` (or `--interactive`) | `U3 VALIDATE` |\n| `U2 APPLY` | `tracked_apply` | rollback tracking requested | `harness init --track` before apply | `U3 VALIDATE` |\n| `U3 VALIDATE` | `checks_pass` | validation ladder passes | run `pnpm check` (+ deep gate if needed) | `U4 DONE` |\n| `U*` | `migration_or_rollback` | contract transition needed | `harness init --migrate` or `harness init --rollback` | `U0 CHECK` |\n| `U*` | `error` | any guard fails | capture blocker + stop | `UX FAIL` |\n\n## Error Handling\n- `VALIDATION_ERROR`: invalid command invocation or malformed workflow input.\n- `BLOCKED_DEPENDENCY`: missing binaries/auth/dependencies required by guards.\n- `POLICY_FAIL`: required validation/policy gates fail.\n- `SYSTEM_ERROR`: runtime/process failures.\n\n## Idempotency\n- Key: `<lane>|<repo>|<event>|<target_state>`.\n- Re-running preview and validate steps must be side-effect free.\n- Apply steps should be replay-safe with guard checks.\n\n## Execution Modes\n- `STRICT`: stop on first violation/failure.\n- `ADVISORY`: collect warnings and continue where safe.\n\n## Dry-Run Simulation\n- No file mutations or external writes; no side effects.\n- Deterministic transition trace output based on guard evaluation.\n\n## Observability Logs\n`workflow_id, transition_code, from_state, to_state, correlation_id, result`\n\n## Validation Checklist\n- every non-terminal state has >=1 outbound transition\n- deterministic `(S,E)` handling\n- failure paths route to `FAIL`/`BLOCKED`\n- terminal states (`B5 READY`, `U4 DONE`) have no outbound transitions\n\n## Executor pseudocode\n\n```txt\nrun bootstrap or update lane\nfor each transition row, execute action when guard is true\nstop on first error transition and emit unblock guidance\n```\n\n## Validation ladder\n\nBaseline gate:\n\n```bash\npnpm check\n```\n\nDeep gate (when runtime behavior, contract behavior, or artifact handling changed):\n\n```bash\npnpm test:deep\n```\n\nSituational governance checks:\n\n```bash\nharness check-authz --contract harness.contract.json --repo <owner/repo> --branch <branch>\nharness check-environment --contract harness.contract.json --attestation artifacts/check-environment-attestation.json\nharness docs-gate --mode advisory --json\nharness tooling-audit --path <directory> --format table\n```\n\n## environment.toml action sync behavior\n\n`harness init` generates `.codex/environments/environment.toml` with:\n\n- Setup script block using the detected package manager.\n- truth source\n\nPrefer runtime command help over stale prose snapshots:\n\n```bash\nharness --help\n```\n\nIf harness is not installed globally in the current shell, use:\n\n```bash\npnpm exec tsx src/cli.ts --help\n```\n\n## Install modes\n\n### Global npm install (required for generated preflight)\n\n```bash\nnpm install -g @brainwav/coding-harness\nharness --help\n```\n\nRecommended managed install:\n\n```bash\nmise install -g npm:@brainwav/coding-harness\n```\n\nRequired private package auth wiring:\n\n```bash\nexport NPM_TOKEN=<token>\n# CircleCI project settings -> Environment Variables:\n#   NPM_TOKEN\n```\n\n### Project-local source CLI (only for coding-harness development)\n\nUse this only when developing inside the `coding-harness` source repository itself.\nGenerated `scripts/check-environment.sh` in consumer repositories does not fall back\nto `pnpm exec tsx src/cli.ts ...`; it requires the global `harness` binary from npm.\n\n## Bootstrap workflow\n\n1. Install dependencies:\n\n## Abbreviations\n| Abbr | Meaning |\n| --- | --- |\n| `S` | state |\n| `E` | event |\n| `G` | guard |\n| `A` | action |\n| `N` | next state |\n\n## Metadata\n| Field | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| `owner` | `TODO` |\n| `max_duration` | `TODO` |\n| `escalation` | `TODO` |\n\n## Invariants\n- TODO invariant 1\n- TODO invariant 2\n\n## States\n```txt\nS0 (non-terminal)\nDONE (terminal)\nFAIL (terminal)\nBLOCKED (terminal)\n```\n\n## Transition Table (Canonical)\n`S | E | G | A | N`\n\n### State machine\n\n```txt\nB0 PREFLIGHT -> B1 DEPS -> B2 INIT_PREVIEW -> B3 INIT_APPLY -> B4 VALIDATE -> B5 READY\n     |             |            |              |\n     +-----------> BX FAIL <----+--------------+\n```\n\n### Transition table (`S | E | G | A | N`)\n\n| S | E | G | A | N |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `B0 PREFLIGHT` | `preflight_ok` | preflight script passes | `source scripts/codex-preflight.sh && preflight_repo` | `B1 DEPS` |\n| `B1 DEPS` | `deps_installed` | `pnpm` available | `pnpm install` | `B2 INIT_PREVIEW` |\n| `B2 INIT_PREVIEW` | `preview_ok` | dry-run exits clean | `harness init --dry-run` | `B3 INIT_APPLY` |\n| `B3 INIT_APPLY` | `init_applied` | templates generated | `harness init` | `B4 VALIDATE` |\n| `B4 VALIDATE` | `checks_pass` | baseline gate passes | `pnpm check` and optional `harness verify-greptile` | `B5 READY` |\n| `B*` | `error` | any guard fails | capture blocker + stop | `BX FAIL` |\n\nExpected scaffold lane in `B3`:\n- `.greptile/config.json`\n- `.greptile/rules.md`\n- `.greptile/files.json`\n- `.github/workflows/greptile-review.yml`\n\n## Update workflow for existing repos\n\n### State machine\n\n```txt\nU0 CHECK -> U1 PREVIEW -> U2 APPLY -> U3 VALIDATE -> U4 DONE\n   |          |           |             |\n   +--------> UX FAIL <---+-------------+\n```\n\n### Transition table (`S | E | G | A | N`)\n\n| S | E | G | A | N |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `U0 CHECK` | `updates_found` | update check runs | `harness init --check-updates` | `U1 PREVIEW` |\n| `U1 PREVIEW` | `preview_ok` | dry-run output reviewed | `harness init --dry-run --update` | `U2 APPLY` |\n| `U2 APPLY` | `apply_ok` | selected update strategy confirmed | `harness init --update` (or `--interactive`) | `U3 VALIDATE` |\n| `U2 APPLY` | `tracked_apply` | rollback tracking requested | `harness init --track` before apply | `U3 VALIDATE` |\n| `U3 VALIDATE` | `checks_pass` | validation ladder passes | run `pnpm check` (+ deep gate if needed) | `U4 DONE` |\n| `U*` | `migration_or_rollback` | contract transition needed | `harness init --migrate` or `harness init --rollback` | `U0 CHECK` |\n| `U*` | `error` | any guard fails | capture blocker + stop | `UX FAIL` |\n\n## Error Handling\n- `VALIDATION_ERROR`: invalid command invocation or malformed workflow input.\n- `BLOCKED_DEPENDENCY`: missing binaries/auth/dependencies required by guards.\n- `POLICY_FAIL`: required validation/policy gates fail.\n- `SYSTEM_ERROR`: runtime/process failures.\n\n## Idempotency\n- Key: `<lane>|<repo>|<event>|<target_state>`.\n- Re-running preview and validate steps must be side-effect free.\n- Apply steps should be replay-safe with guard checks.\n\n## Execution Modes\n- `STRICT`: stop on first violation/failure.\n- `ADVISORY`: collect warnings and continue where safe.\n\n## Dry-Run Simulation\n- No file mutations or external writes; no side effects.\n- Deterministic transition trace output based on guard evaluation.\n\n## Observability Logs\n`workflow_id, transition_code, from_state, to_state, correlation_id, result`\n\n## Validation Checklist\n- every non-terminal state has >=1 outbound transition\n- deterministic `(S,E)` handling\n- failure paths route to `FAIL`/`BLOCKED`\n- terminal states (`B5 READY`, `U4 DONE`) have no outbound transitions\n\n## Executor pseudocode\n\n```txt\nrun bootstrap or update lane\nfor each transition row, execute action when guard is true\nstop on first error transition and emit unblock guidance\n```\n\n## Validation ladder\n\nBaseline gate:\n\n```bash\npnpm check\n```\n\nDeep gate (when runtime behavior, contract behavior, or artifact handling changed):\n\n```bash\npnpm test:deep\n```\n\nSituational governance checks:\n\n```bash\nharness check-authz --contract harness.contract.json --repo <owner/repo> --branch <branch>\nharness check-environment --contract harness.contract.json --attestation artifacts/check-environment-attestation.json\nharness docs-gate --mode advisory --json\nharness tooling-audit --path <directory> --format table\n```\n\n## environment.toml action sync behavior\n\n`harness init` generates `.codex/environments/environment.toml` with:\n\n- Setup script block using the detected package manager.\n- `test`\n\nImportant constraints:\n\n- Auto-update is safe by default only for harness-autogenerated files.\n- Existing custom/non-autogenerated environment files should be treated as user-owned and If any are missing, surface the gap and stop.\n\n| Requirement | Check command | Required |\n|---|---|---|\n| `node` ≥ 24 | `node --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `pnpm` ≥ 10 | `pnpm --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `git` | `git --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `jq` | `jq --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `rg` (ripgrep) | `rg --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `fd` | `fd --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `mise` | `mise --version` | ✅ mandatory |\n| `gh` CLI | `gh --version` | Required for branch-protect + GitHub steps |\n| `NPM_TOKEN` env var | `printenv NPM_TOKEN \\| wc -c` (must be > 1) | Required for private pkg auth |\n| 1Password session | `op account list` | Required if secrets come from 1Password |\n\n```bash\n# Quick preflight — run this first\nnode --version && pnpm --version && git --version && jq --version && rg --version && fd --version && mise --version\nprintenv NPM_TOKEN | wc -c   # must print a number > 1\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: Auth and private registry\n\nharness is a **private** npm package. Auth must be wired before install.\n\n```bash\n# 1. Confirm token is present (do NOT print the value)\nprintenv NPM_TOKEN | wc -c\n\n# 2. Wire token into ~/.npmrc\necho \"//registry.npmjs.org/:_authToken=${NPM_TOKEN}\" >> ~/.npmrc\n\n# 3. Verify npm can authenticate\nnpm whoami\n\n# 4. Confirm the package is reachable\nnpm view @brainwav/coding-harness dist-tags --json\n```\n\nIf `npm whoami` fails, the session NPM_TOKEN is stale or invalid. Stop and instruct the\nuser to refresh their 1Password session or re-export the token.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Install harness globally\n\n```bash\n# Preferred: managed via mise (pinned version, login-shell accessible)\nmise install -g npm:@brainwav/coding-harness\n\n# Fallback: direct npm global (same effect)\nnpm install -g @brainwav/coding-harness\n\n# Verify\nharness --version\nharness --help\n```\n\n> **Do not** use `pnpm exec tsx src/cli.ts` in a consumer repo. That path is\n> coding-harness development only. Generated `scripts/check-environment.sh` always\n> expects the global `harness` binary.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: Init scaffold\n\nThe `harness init` command scaffolds all governance files. Follow this sequence:\n\n```bash\n# 3.1 — Dry run first (no writes — safe to always run)\nharness init --dry-run\n\n# Review the planned files. If anything looks wrong, STOP.\n\n# 3.2 — Apply (CircleCI is the standard CI provider for this project)\nharness init\n\n# 3.3 — Verify scaffold applied\nharness init --check-updates\n```\n\n> **CI provider:** `circleci` is the default. Do **not** pass `--ci circleci` here:\n> the current CLI treats that extra positional argument as a target directory.\n> Greptile review runs as a **CircleCI job**\n> (`greptile-review`) — no GitHub Actions workflow file is written or required.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3b: Migrate away from GitHub Actions\n\n**Skip this phase if the project has no `.github/workflows/` directory.**\n\nIf the target repo already has GitHub Actions workflows, use the dedicated migration\ncommand to remove them safely and cut over to CircleCI. This is a staged, reversible\nprocess — do not manually delete workflow files.\n\n```bash\n# 3b.1 — Preview what will change (safe, no writes)\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --dry-run\n```\n\nReview the output. Confirm the listed `.github/workflows/` files will be removed and\nthat the CircleCI config is already present (from Phase 3). If anything looks wrong, STOP.\n\n```bash\n# 3b.2 — Apply the migration and create the recovery snapshot\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --apply\n\n# 3b.3 — Verify migration is complete (uses the snapshot id from prepare output)\nharness ci-migrate verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n\n# 3b.4 — Commit the cutover once verification passes\nharness ci-migrate commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n```\n\n**If something goes wrong:**\n\n```bash\n# Roll back to the pre-migration state\nharness ci-migrate abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n```\n\n**What `ci-migrate` does:**\n- Removes `.github/workflows/` CI job files that conflict with CircleCI\n- Updates `harness.contract.json` `activeProvider` to `circleci`\n- Updates `.harness/ci-required-checks.json` to CircleCI check names\n- Creates a snapshot so rollback is always available\n- Does **not** touch `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, `CODEOWNERS`, or `ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` — those are PR hygiene, not CI\n\n> **Agent rule:** Never manually delete `.github/workflows/` files. Always use\n> `harness ci-migrate` so the snapshot + rollback path is available.\n\n**Key files every project MUST have after init:**\n\n```\nharness.contract.json               — governance contract (source of truth)\n.harness/ci-required-checks.json    — required CI check list\n.greptile/config.json               — Greptile AI review config (repo-scoped)\n.greptile/rules.md                  — Greptile custom rules\n.greptile/files.json                — Greptile context files\n.circleci/config.yml                — All CI jobs (lint, test, security, greptile-review, release)\n.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md    — PR checklist\nscripts/codex-preflight.sh          — agent preflight script (source at session start)\nscripts/check-environment.sh        — harness env readiness script\n.codex/environments/environment.toml — Codex action blocks\n.mise.toml                          — pinned tool versions\nMakefile                            — canonical task runner\nprek.toml                           — pre-commit/pre-push hook config\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4: mise tool baseline\n\nAfter init, harness scaffolds `.mise.toml` with required tool versions.\nInstall everything it pins:\n\n```bash\n# 4.1 — Trust the mise config (required first time)\nmise trust .mise.toml\n\n# 4.2 — Install all tools\nmise install\n\n# 4.3 — Verify key binaries are present\nmise list\nharness check-environment --json\n```\n\n**If `harness check-environment` reports missing binaries**, install them:\n\n```bash\nmise install -g cargo:prek\nmise install -g npm:@brainwav/diagram\nmise install -g npm:@argos-ci/cli\nmise install -g npm:@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli\nmise install -g npm:agentation\nmise install -g npm:agentation-mcp\nmise install -g npm:agent-browser\nmise install -g npm:beautiful-mermaid\nmise install -g npm:markdownlint-cli2\nmise install -g npm:wrangler\n```\n\nVerify binaries are reachable in login shells (non-interactive PATH must be correct):\n\n```bash\nzsh -lc 'prek --version && diagram --version && mmdc --version'\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 5: CircleCI environment variables and GitHub secrets\n\n### CircleCI project environment variables\n\nThese are set in **CircleCI project settings → Environment Variables**.\nHarness cannot create them — surface this to the user if any are missing.\n\n| Variable | Purpose | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| `NPM_TOKEN` | Install `@brainwav/coding-harness` in CI jobs | npm / 1Password |\n| `GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` | `gh` CLI auth for review-gate, PR creation, branch ops | GitHub PAT |\n| `GREPTILE_API_KEY` | Greptile API call in the `greptile-review` CircleCI job | Greptile dashboard |\n| `LINEAR_API_KEY` | Linear issue sync job | Linear settings |\n\nCheck via CircleCI API:\n\n```bash\n# Replace <owner> and <repo>; requires CIRCLE_TOKEN env var\ncurl -s --request GET \\\n  --url \"https://circleci.com/api/v2/project/github/<owner>/<repo>/envvar\" \\\n  --header \"Circle-Token: $CIRCLE_TOKEN\" | jq '[.items[].name]'\n```\n\n> **No GitHub Actions secrets are required.** All CI including Greptile runs through\n> CircleCI. The only GitHub-side configuration is the Greptile GitHub App installation.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 6: Branch protection\n\n```bash\n# Apply branch protection using the harness ecosystem profile\nharness branch-protect \\\n  --token \"$GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN\" \\\n  --owner <owner> \\\n  --repo <repo> \\\n  --ecosystem harness \\\n  --branch main"}],"structured_content":{"content":"# Agent install guide — @brainwav/coding-harness in a consumer project\n\n> **For AI agents.** Follow every step in order. If anything looks wrong, STOP.\n\n```bash\n# 3b.2 — Apply the migration and create the recovery snapshot\nharness ci-migrate prepare --provider circleci --apply\n\n# 3b.3 — Verify migration is complete (uses the snapshot id from prepare output)\nharness ci-migrate verify --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n\n# 3b.4 — Commit the cutover once verification passes\nharness ci-migrate commit --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n```\n\n**If something goes wrong:**\n\n```bash\n# Roll back to the pre-migration state\nharness ci-migrate abort --snapshot <snapshot-id>\n```\n\n**What `ci-migrate` does:**\n- Removes `.github/workflows/` CI job files that conflict with CircleCI\n- Updates `harness.contract.json` `activeProvider` to `circleci`\n- Updates `.harness/ci-required-checks.json` to CircleCI check names\n- Creates a snapshot so rollback is always available\n- Does **not** touch `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, `CODEOWNERS`, or `ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` — those are PR hygiene, not CI\n\n> **Agent rule:** Never manually delete `.github/workflows/` files. Always use\n> `harness ci-migrate` so the snapshot + rollback path is available.\n\n**Key files every project MUST have after init:**\n\n```\nharness.contract.json               — governance contract readiness script\n.codex/environments/environment.toml — Codex action blocks\n.mise.toml                          — pinned tool versions\nMakefile                            — canonical task runner\nprek.toml                           — pre-commit/pre-push hook config\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4: mise tool baseline\n\nAfter init, harness scaffolds `.mise.toml` with required tool {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_12","type":"agent_message","text":"For the harness, you’d usually prefer a GitHub App JWT when the question is specifically “is this app installed here, and can this installation act on this repo?” because a JWT proves the app’s identity, not a human user’s identity.\n\nA PAT answers a different question. A PAT says “this user can access the repo,” which can mask the exact thing you’re trying to verify. If `harness verify-greptile` succeeds with a PAT, you still haven’t cleanly proven that the GitHub App installation itself is present and authorized. A GitHub App JWT is the right starting credential for installation-scoped verification, because it maps to the app, can be exchanged for an installation token, and keeps the check aligned with the actual production actor.\n\nWhy that’s better in practice:\n- Least privilege: the resulting installation token is scoped to the app installation and granted permissions, instead of inheriting a user PAT’s broader repo/org reach.\n- Better signal: success means “the app installation is valid for this repo,” not merely “some maintainer’s PAT can see this repo.”\n- Cleaner ops: JWTs are short-lived and app-managed, so they’re safer to automate than long-lived PATs.\n- Better governance: orgs often restrict PAT usage more aggressively than GitHub Apps, and app auth is easier to reason about in audits.\n\nThe nuance in this repo is that the docs are a little mixed. The harness reference explicitly says GitHub App JWT is preferred for remote GitHub checks when verifying app installation state in [utilities/coding-harness/references/setup-and-commands.md](/Users/jamiecraik/dev/Agent-Skills/utilities/coding-harness/references/setup-and-commands.md), but the install guide still shows PAT-based examples for `verify-greptile` and other GitHub operations in [utilities/coding-harness/references/agent-install-guide.md](/Users/jamiecraik/dev/Agent-Skills/utilities/coding-harness/references/agent-install-guide.md). My read is: PAT is still accepted because it’s convenient and also needed for some user-level tasks like `gh` CLI auth and branch protection, but for verifying app-installation access specifically, JWT is the more correct credential.\n\nA simple rule of thumb:\n- Use GitHub App JWT when verifying GitHub App installation state.\n- Use PAT for user-operated repo/admin flows like `gh` CLI actions, branch protection, or manual maintenance steps.\n\nIf you want, I can also turn this into a short “when to use PAT vs JWT” note for the harness docs, since the current guidance looks slightly inconsistent."}}
